         Section 4.       Title Objections. No later than twenty (20) calendar days prior to Closing,
Purchaser shall notify Seller in writing of any encumbrances reflected on the Title Commitment, Survey,
any other customary third-party report, or any updates thereto that do not constitute Permitted Exceptions
and are not acceptable to the Purchaser (such exceptions referred to herein as the “Title Objections”) (which
for the avoidance of doubt, shall include any zoning or parking compliance matters). Purchaser shall have
five (5) Business Days following receipt of any amended or updated Title Commitment, Survey, or other
third-party report in which to provide updated Title Objections to Seller. If all Title Objections are capable
of being cured by Seller by expending no more than Three Hundred Thousand Dollars ($300,000.00) (the
“Must-Cure Amount”), Seller shall remove all such Title Objections at its sole expense. Seller shall have
five (5) Business Days following receipt of notice of any initial or subsequent Title Objections to notify
Purchaser in writing either (i) of its acceptance of the obligation to cure such Title Objections, or (ii) if the
cost to cure exceeds the Must-Cure Amount, of its election either to attempt to cure such Title Objections
prior to the Closing or decline to attempt to cure such Title Objections. Seller’s failure to give such notice
on a timely basis shall be deemed its election to not attempt to cure such Title Objections. If, in accordance
with the foregoing, Seller elects to not attempt to cure such Title Objections, Purchaser may, at its option:
(i) terminate this Agreement, in which event the Deposit, plus all accrued interest, shall be returned to
Purchaser and whereupon the Parties shall have no further obligations hereunder (except those that
expressly survive termination); or (ii) proceed to Closing, in which case such Title Objections shall be
deemed waived and become Permitted Exceptions, and the Purchase Price shall be reduced by the Must-
Cure Amount. If Seller elects to attempt to cure a Title Objection, completion of such cure shall be a
condition of Purchaser’s obligation to consummate the Closing. Notwithstanding anything to the contrary
herein, whether or not included in the Title Objections, Seller shall be obligated to cure, or to cause Old
Operator to cure, at or prior to Closing all encumbrances affecting the Property (other than liens solely
arising from and related to the HUD Loans) that may be removed by the payment of money, including (A)
liens securing a mortgage, deed of trust or trust deed; (B) tax or judgment liens; (C) broker’s liens; and (D)
any mechanics’ or materialman’s liens.

         Section 5.       Conditions to Closing.

         Section 6.       Apportionments; Post-Closing.

                  (a)     Closing Prorations. All items of income and expense relating to the Purchased
          Assets or the operations of the Facility attributable to periods both before and after the Closing
          Date shall be apportioned at the Closing. The Parties shall pro-rate the following as of 12:01 a.m.
          on the day of the Closing on the basis of the actual number of calendar days of the month which
          shall have elapsed as of the Closing and based upon the actual number of calendar days in the
          month and a 365 or 366 day year, as the case may be. In connection with the foregoing, the Parties
          agree to make the following apportionments:



                                                        9

4900-6014-8071, v. 18

===== PAGE 59 =====
                           (i)      Real estate taxes, assessments, personal property taxes, and water, vault
                  and sewer charges and rents, as well as any other governmental charges or taxes assessed
                  on or in relation to the Purchased Assets, the Facility, or the Personal Property (as defined
                  in the OTA), based on the rates and assessed valuation applicable in the fiscal year for
                  which assessed; Seller and Purchaser agree to re-prorate taxes upon Purchaser’s receipt of
                  the actual tax bill for the tax year in question if the actual bills differ from the bills upon
                  which the prorations are based as a result of a tax complaint, revaluation or otherwise. This
                  obligation to re-prorate shall survive the Closing. Allocation of real estate taxes billed with
                  respect to the Property to yearly periods shall be determined in accordance with local
                  custom, as determined by the Title Company. If, prior to the Closing, the Property or any
                  portion thereof shall be affected by any special or general assessments which are or may
                  become payable in installments, Seller shall (i) so inform Purchaser and (ii) pay the unpaid
                  installments of such assessments which are due prior to the Closing and Purchaser shall
                  pay the installments which are due on or after the Closing.

                           (ii)     All charges and payments for utility services; provided, however, that if
                  there is no meter or if the current bill for any of such utilities has not been issued prior to
                  the Closing Date, then such charges shall be adjusted at the Closing on the basis of the
                  charges for the prior period for which bills were issued and shall be further adjusted when
                  the bills for the current period are issued; provided, further, to the extent possible, Seller
                  shall, or shall cause Old Operator to, terminate the accounts with the utility service
                  providers associated with the Facility and Purchaser shall, or shall cause New Operator to,
                  establish new accounts with such utility service providers effective on the Closing Date, in
                  which event, there shall be no proration for such utility services.

                          (iii)   All amounts allocable to periods prior to Closing pursuant to this Section
                  6(a) shall be paid and discharged by Seller at or prior to Closing (except as expressly
                  provided otherwise).

                  (b)    Insurance Policies. Unless otherwise agreed, no insurance policies of Seller are to
          be transferred to Purchaser, and no apportionment of the premiums therefor shall be made, in
          which event, Purchaser shall be responsible for securing its own insurance for the Property.

                  (c)     Survival. The obligations of the Parties under this Section 6 shall survive the
          Closing.

         Section 7.       Interim Period.

                   (a)     From the Effective Date until Closing, Seller shall, unless consented to otherwise
          by Purchaser in writing: (a) maintain the Purchased Assets in substantially the same condition as
          they existed on the Effective Date, reasonable wear and tear excepted; (b) during normal business
          hours and upon reasonable prior notice, or at any time during the forty-eight (48) hours prior to
          the Closing, permit Purchaser and its Representatives to inspect the Property and the other
          Purchased Assets, and, subject to applicable law, to examine Seller’s such books and records that
          are included in the Purchased Assets or that will otherwise be transferred to Purchaser at Closing,
          subject to the restrictions set forth in Section 3(b) with respect to the access thereunder; (c) other
          than to the extent required for the operation of the Facility, not enter into any contract that is not
          terminable at Closing without penalty, or that could otherwise become an obligation of Purchaser;
          (d) not create, or allow to exist (other than as will be cleared at Closing) any Exception upon or
          affecting title to the Property or the Purchased Assets except Permitted Exceptions; (e) other than
          to the extent required for the operation of the Facility, not take any action which would or could
          reasonably be expected to cause any of the representations or warranties in this Agreement to

          proceed; and, if such efforts are unsuccessful, (ii) Purchaser shall seek alternative financing on

         Section 10.      Risk of Loss.

                   (a)     Fire or Other Casualty. The risk of any loss or damage to any of the Purchased
          Assets by fire or other casualty before the Closing hereunder is assumed by Seller. Seller shall
          notify Purchaser in writing of any fire or other casualty within three (3) calendar days of the
          occurrence of such fire or other casualty (but in any event prior to Closing), which notice shall
          include a description thereof in reasonable detail and an estimate of the cost of and time to repair.
          If the Purchased Assets shall suffer any fire or other casualty or any injury and Purchaser does not
          (or is not permitted to) elect to cancel this Agreement as hereinafter provided, Seller agrees to
          repair the damage at its sole cost and expense before the date set for Closing hereunder in a manner
          deemed acceptable to Purchaser in its reasonable discretion, or, in the alternative at Purchaser’s
          sole option, the Purchase Price shall be reduced by an amount equal to the amount needed to repair
          such damage as estimated by a third party contractor acceptable to Purchaser and Seller in their
          reasonable discretion. In the event of any material damage or destruction of the Property,
          Purchaser, within ten (10) calendar days after notice thereof by Seller is received by Purchaser,
          shall have the option, by written notice to Seller, to terminate this Agreement. For purposes of
          this Section 10(a), “material damage or destruction” means any damage or destruction to the
          Property that would require the expenditure of an amount greater than Three Hundred Fifty
          Thousand Dollars ($350,000.00) to repair (including in said amount the amount of any revenue
          lost as a result of said fire or other casualty). If Purchaser so elects to cancel this Agreement, the
          Deposit plus all accrued interest shall be returned to Purchaser and all Parties shall be relieved of
          any further obligations or liabilities hereunder except for obligations which expressly survive the
          termination of this Agreement.

                   (b)    Eminent Domain. The risk of any loss or damage to the Property by condemnation
          before the Closing Date hereunder is assumed by Seller. Seller shall notify Purchaser in writing,
          in the event any condemnation proceeding is commenced or threatened, within three (3) calendar
          days after the occurrence of such commencement or threat (but in any event prior to Closing),
          together with such reasonable details with respect thereto as to which Seller has knowledge. As
          soon as the portion or portions of the Property to be taken are reasonably determinable, Seller shall
          so notify Purchaser in writing, which shall include Seller’s estimate of the value of the portion or
          portions of the Property to be so taken. In the event of any material taking of the Property,
          Purchaser, within ten (10) calendar days after notice thereof by Seller is received by Purchaser,


                                                      16

===== PAGE 66 =====
          shall have the option, by written notice to Seller, to terminate this Agreement, in which event the
          Deposit plus all accrued interest shall be returned to Purchaser and all Parties shall be relieved of
          any further obligations or liabilities hereunder except for obligations which expressly survive the
          termination of this Agreement. If less than a material taking occurs or would result as of such
          notice, Purchaser shall proceed with the Closing and take the Improvements (with the Purchased
          Assets), together with all related awards or the right to receive same. For the purposes of this
          Section 10(b), a “material taking” means: (i) any taking of the Property, (A) the effect of which
          would be to require the expenditure of an amount greater than Three Hundred Fifty Thousand
          Dollars ($350,000.00) to repair the balance of the Property, or (B) which would or could
          reasonably be expected to materially impair the use or operation of the Purchased Assets; or (ii)
          any threat of a taking or any reasonably equivalent indication on the part of a condemning
          authority of such intention where there is no reasonable basis to conclude that the actual taking
          would not be material. If Purchaser shall not so elect to cancel this Agreement, then the sale of
          the Property shall be consummated at the Purchase Price provided for herein (without abatement)
          and Seller shall assign to Purchaser at the Closing all of Seller’s right, title, and interest in and to

         Section 11.      Indemnification.

                    (a)    By Purchaser. Purchaser shall indemnify, save, protect, defend, and hold harmless
          Seller, its employees, affiliates, members, managers, shareholders, officers, directors, and agents,
          and each of their successors and assigns, from and against all claims, liabilities, losses, damages,
          demands, and causes of action of any nature whatsoever (including demands and causes of action
          relating to injury or death to persons or loss of or damage to property), and all costs and expenses
          (including penalties and reasonable attorneys’ and other professional fees and disbursements
          incurred in the investigation or defense of any such claims, or in asserting, pursuing, or enforcing
          any such claims), whether or not resulting from third-party claims (collectively, “Losses”) arising
          from, out of, or relating to: (i) Purchaser’s ownership of the Purchased Assets or the operation of
          the Facility by Purchaser or New Operator, as applicable, on or after the Closing Date; (ii)
          Purchaser’s use or occupancy of the Property or the condition thereof on or after the Closing Date;
          and (iii) any inaccuracy or breach of any representation, warranty, covenant, agreement, or
          obligation contained in this Agreement, or in any of the Other Documents (without regard to any
          qualification or limitation contained therein as to materiality or Purchaser’s knowledge).
          Purchaser further agrees to pay any reasonable attorneys’ fees and expenses of Seller arising from
          any indemnification obligation hereunder.

                   (b)     By Seller. Seller agrees to indemnify, save, protect, defend, and hold harmless
          Purchaser and its affiliates, and each of their respective employees, members, managers, members,
          officers, directors, and agents, and each of their successors and assigns, from and against all Losses
          arising from, out of, or relating to: (i) Seller’s ownership of the Purchased Assets prior to the
          Closing Date; (ii) any inaccuracy or breach of any representation, warranty, covenant, agreement,
          or obligation contained in this Agreement or in any of the Other Documents (without regard to
          any qualification or limitation contained therein as to materiality or Seller’s knowledge), and (iii)
          any of the Excluded Assets or Retained Liabilities. Seller further agrees to pay any reasonable
          attorneys’ fees and expenses of Purchaser arising from any indemnification obligation hereunder.


                                                       17

4900-6014-8071, v. 18

===== PAGE 67 =====
                   (c)     Method of Indemnification for Third-Party Claims. In the event that any claim or
          demand for Losses which are subject to indemnification by or under any provision of this
          Agreement (“Indemnitee’s Claim”) is made against or received by any indemnified party
          (hereinafter “Indemnitee”) hereunder, such Indemnitee shall notify the indemnifying party
          (hereinafter “Indemnitor”) in writing within thirty (30) calendar days of Indemnitee’s receipt of
          written notice thereof; provided, however, that Indemnitee’s failure to timely notify Indemnitor of
          Indemnitee’s receipt of an Indemnitee’s Claim shall not impair, void, vitiate, or invalidate
          Indemnitee’s right to receive, and Indemnitor’s obligation to provide, indemnification hereunder
          unless such failure directly and materially prejudices Indemnitor’s right or ability to defend the
          Indemnitee’s Claim. Upon receipt of notice of any Indemnitee’s Claim(s), Indemnitor shall,
          subject to Indemnitee’s other rights as set forth herein, diligently and vigorously defend,
          compromise or settle said Indemnitee’s Claim at Indemnitor’s sole and exclusive cost and expense
          and shall promptly provide Indemnitee evidence thereof promptly upon the final, unappealable
          resolution of such Indemnitee’s Claim. Indemnitor shall keep Indemnitee informed with respect
          to all aspects of the resolution of an Indemnitee’s Claim. Without limiting the foregoing, upon
          request by Indemnitee, Indemnitor shall within five (5) calendar days: (A) provide Indemnitee a
          true, correct, accurate, and complete written status report regarding the then current status of any
          Indemnitee’s Claim; and (B) file for, and vigorously pursue, the removal as a defendant of any
          Indemnitee who is improperly named in any action underlying an Indemnitee’s Claim. Indemnitor
          shall not, without the prior written consent of Indemnitee, which consent shall not be unreasonably
          withheld, settle, compromise, or offer to settle or compromise any Indemnitee’s Claim on a basis
          that would result in: (i) the imposition of a consent order, injunction, or decree that would restrict
          the future activity or conduct of Indemnitee or any affiliate thereof; or (ii) any monetary liability
          on the part of Indemnitee or any affiliate thereof that will not be paid or reimbursed by Indemnitor.
          Prior to an Indemnification Default (as defined herein), Indemnitee may not settle or compromise
          an Indemnitee’s Claim without Indemnitor’s prior written consent, such consent not to be
          unreasonably withheld or delayed. In the event that Indemnitor fails or refuses to indemnify, save,
          defend, protect, or hold Indemnitee harmless from and against an Indemnitee’s Claim or to
          diligently pursue the same to its conclusion, or in the event that Indemnitor fails to timely report
          to Indemnitee the status of its efforts to reach a final resolution of an Indemnitee’s Claim, upon
          fourteen (14) calendar days’ prior written notice to Indemnitor during which time Indemnitor may
          cure any alleged default hereunder, the foregoing shall immediately, automatically, and without
          further notice be deemed an event of default hereunder (an “Indemnification Default”) and
          thereafter Indemnitee may, but shall not be obligated to, immediately and without notice to
          Indemnitor, except such notice as may be required by law, intervene in and defend, settle, or
          compromise such Indemnitee’s Claim at Indemnitor’s sole and exclusive cost and expense,
          including but not limited to attorneys’ fees, and, thereafter, within fourteen (14) calendar days of
          written demand thereof, Indemnitor shall promptly reimburse Indemnitee all amounts (including
          attorneys’ fees) incurred by Indemnitee with respect to the resolution of such Indemnitee’s Claims.

                   (d)    Method of Indemnification for Direct Claims. If and when an Indemnitee desires
          to assert an Indemnitee’s Claim in accordance with Section 11(a) or Section 11(b), as applicable,
          other than with respect to a third-party claim in accordance with Section 11(c), Indemnitee shall
          notify Indemnitor of such Indemnitee’s Claim and the Parties shall attempt in good faith to agree
          on resolution of the disputed amount. Any amount mutually agreed upon or awarded to the
          Indemnitee under a final judgment shall be paid by Indemnitor within five (5) business days
          following such agreement or the entering of such judgment, as applicable.

                   (e)     Survival. The Parties’ obligations under this Section 11 shall survive the Closing
          for a period of three (3) years; provided, however, that (A) Seller’s obligations under this Section
          11 arising from, related to or with respect to (i) power and authority to enter into this Agreement,

          (ii) any taxes related to the Property, and (iii) the environmental condition of the Property, (B) any
          indemnity related to fraud or intentional misrepresentations shall survive indefinitely, subject to

          breach of any covenant, obligation, or agreement shall survive in accordance with the terms set

    Closing Date, and for Indemnitee’s Claims made thereafter, Seven Hundred and Fifty Thousand Dollars
    ($750,000.00) for month twenty-five (25) through month thirty-six (36) after the Closing Date. For the
    avoidance of doubt, this personal guarantee, including with respect to any Recapture Guarantees of
    Seller or Old Operator shall expire with respect to any Indemnitee’s Claims not yet made on the last
    day of the thirty sixth (36th) month after the Closing Date and Guarantor shall thereafter have no further

            (b) For all other indemnification obligations of Seller Parties, the aggregate liability of
    Guarantor to Purchaser Parties shall not exceed Five Hundred Thousand Dollars ($500,000.00) for
    Indemnitee’s Claims made during first eighteen (18) months after the Closing Date. For the avoidance
    of doubt, this personal guarantee with respect to any Indemnitee’s Claims not yet made concerning
    indemnification obligations that are not Recapture Guarantees shall expire on the last day of the
    eighteenth (18th) month after the Closing Date and Guarantor shall thereafter have no further
    obligations to Purchaser Parties in connection thereto.

             (c) Except as expressly provided herein, Guarantor’s obligations under this Section are
    absolute and unconditional, irrespective of (i) any amendment, modification, or waiver of any provision
    of this Agreement or the OTA, (ii) any extension of time for performance or payment granted to Seller
    or Old Operator, (iii) any bankruptcy, insolvency, or similar proceeding affecting Seller, Old Operator,
    or any other Person, or (iv) any other circumstance that might otherwise constitute a legal or equitable
    discharge or defense of a guarantor. For the avoidance of doubt, Guarantor’s obligation with respect to
    any Indemnitee’s Claim shall be determined solely based on the date such Indemnitee’s Claim was first
    made, and no time-based expiration or reduction of Guarantor’s liability set forth in Section 11(a) or
    Section 11(b) shall apply to such Indemnitee’s Claim once made.

          ISLAND, OR, IF FEDERAL JURISDICTION EXISTS, THE FEDERAL COURT HAVING
          SITUS IN THE STATE OF RHODE ISLAND. EACH OF THE PARTIES HEREBY
          CONSENTS AND SUBMITS TO THE JURISDICTION OF SUCH LOCAL, STATE OR
          FEDERAL COURTS. TO THE EXTENT LEGALLY WAIVABLE, EACH OF THE PARTIES
          HEREBY WAIVES PERSONAL SERVICE OF ANY AND ALL PROCESS AND AGREES
          THAT ALL SUCH SERVICE OF PROCESS MAY BE MADE UPON SUCH PARTIES BY
          CERTIFIED OR REGISTERED MAIL, RETURN RECEIPT REQUESTED, ADDRESSED TO
          SUCH PARTY, AT THE ADDRESS SET FORTH FOR NOTICE IN THIS AGREEMENT AND
          SERVICE SO MADE SHALL BE COMPLETE TEN (10) CALENDAR DAYS AFTER THE
          SAME HAS BEEN POSTED. THE PARTIES HEREBY WAIVE ANY RIGHT THEY MAY
          HAVE TO TRANSFER OR CHANGE THE VENUE OF ANY LITIGATION BROUGHT
          AGAINST SUCH PARTY IN ACCORDANCE WITH THIS SECTION. THE PARTIES
          HEREBY WAIVE ANY RIGHT THEY MAY HAVE TO A TRIAL BY JURY.

                   (i)    Reserved.

                  (j)    Headings. The headings of the various sections of this Agreement have been
          inserted only for the purposes of convenience, are not part of this Agreement, and shall not be
          deemed in any manner to modify, explain, qualify, or restrict any of the provisions of this
          Agreement.

                  (k)     Counterparts. This Agreement may be executed in any number of counterparts,
          each of which shall for all purposes be deemed an original, but all of such counterparts shall
          together constitute one and the same agreement. Signatures sent by telecopy or electronic mail
          transmissions (specifically including DocuSign) shall constitute originals.

                    (l)    Successors and Assigns. This Agreement shall bind and inure to the benefit of the
          respective heirs, executors, administrators, personal representatives, successors, and permitted
          assigns of the Parties; provided, however, that neither Party shall assign this Agreement without
          the prior written consent of the other Party. Notwithstanding the foregoing, Purchaser may assign
          all or a portion of its rights under this Agreement to an affiliated or related entity or entities. Any
          assignment not permitted hereunder and undertaken without such prior written consent shall be
          deemed null and void.

                   (m) Further Assurances. Each Party agrees to use commercially reasonable efforts to
          cause the conditions to its obligations and to the other Party’s obligations herein set forth to be
          satisfied at or prior to the Closing Date. Each of Seller and Purchaser shall provide to the other
          such further assurances as may reasonably be required hereunder to effectuate the purposes of this
          Agreement and, without limiting the foregoing, shall execute and deliver such affidavits,
          certificates, and other instruments as may be so required hereunder so long as the same shall not
          materially increase the liability of the Party so executing and delivering said instrument. Each
          Party shall promptly notify the other Party of any information delivered to or obtained by such

                                    DUE DILIGENCE MATERIALS

                                       Due Diligence Request List

        Please provide all documents, information and materials responsive to the below requests with
respect to (i) South County Nursing and Rehabilitation Center (the “Business”), (ii) 740 Oak Hill Road,
North Kingstown, RI 02852 (the “Property”) and (iii) any entity (all such entities, collectively, the
“Company”) that owns or operates the Property, the Business, or any material asset of the Business. To the
extent an item is not applicable or has already been provided, please so indicate.

1. Corporate:
      a. Provide the (i) corporate name (and any d/b/a) of each entity constituting a part of the Company
          and (ii) name and address of each equityholder of each entity constituting a part of the
          Company.
      b. If the Company includes subsidiaries or other affiliated entities, provide a current
          organizational chart of the Company and describe the nature of the relationships.

2. Contracts:
      a. Provide all material contracts, including each agreement (i) with any payor, (ii) with any
          material vendor or supplier, (iii) with any union or relating to any pension plan or (iv) limiting
          the right of the Company or the Business to conduct any part of the Business in any manner or
          area or otherwise restricting the activities of the Company or the Business.
      b. Provide copies of any form agreements used by the Business.

3. Financial:
       a. Provide any recent (i) budgets and business plans and (ii) tax returns and financials.
       b. Provide a schedule of all (i) debts and financing of the Company or the Business and related
           loan documents and (ii) all loans made by the Company or the Business and related
           documentation. Provide current principal balance, interest rate and escrow amounts of any
           loan insured by HUD, as well as all underlying documentation and material correspondences.

4. Employment: Provide all employment agreements, union contracts, consulting agreements, benefit
   plans (including all information with respect to any Company or Business pension plans), manuals and
   similar items, as well as a list of all employees and consultants, including hire dates, salaries and
   positions.

5. Regulatory:
      a. Provide the number of beds the Facility is licensed for.
      b. List each license and regulatory approval held by the Business or the Company. Provide all
          agreements and material correspondence with any regulatory body, any other governmental
          entity and any payor, in each case, during the prior four (4) years.
      c. Provide three-year survey history.

6. Litigation: Provide all documentation related to any pending or threatened litigation, or any litigation
   that has been completed in the prior four (4) years. Include potential claims, such as a direct threat to
   sue, a request for records, residents who left because of dissatisfaction, injuries and illnesses to
   residents attributable to facility staff or physical plant going back at least 6 months.

===== PAGE 79 =====
7. Intellectual Property and Information Security: List any domain names owned or used by the Business
   or the Company. Provide all other information related to intellectual property (including trade names,
   trademarks, phone numbers, royalty or licensing agreements) of the Business or the Company.

8. Real Property: List of any real property owned or leased by the Company (together with an indication
   whether such property is owned or leased) and any mortgages, lease agreements, deeds, title reports
   and policies, and all other material documents encumbering or otherwise relating to the Property or
   any other real property owned or leased by the Company or the Business. Provide most recent title
   insurance policy, survey, property condition, zoning and environmental reports.

9. Miscellaneous:
      a. List any related party transactions and provide all underlying documents.
      b. Provide (i) a list of all insurance policies and (ii) loss runs for the prior four years.
      c. Provide copies of, in the case of written agreements, and a schedule of, in the case of oral
           agreements, any other significant agreements (e.g., those that provide for material amounts
           payable to or from the Business, acquisitions, dispositions or joint ventures, and any
           agreements, that if breached or terminated would materially impact the Business), and any
           other documents or information which could be significant in assessing the financial and
           operational condition of the Business or the Company.

         WHEREAS, 780 Oak Hill Road Eden Realty LLC, a Rhode Island limited liability company and
an affiliate of Old Operator (“Seller”), and South County Propco LLC, a Delaware limited liability company
and an affiliate of New Operator (“Purchaser”) have entered into that certain Asset Purchase Agreement
(the “APA”), dated as of the date hereof, pursuant to which Purchaser will purchase that certain real
property (as more fully described on Exhibit A to the APA, the “Real Property”) improved with that certain
120-bed skilled nursing facility commonly known as “South County Nursing & Rehabilitation Center” and
located at 740 Oak Hill Road, North Kingstown, RI 02852 (the “Facility”);

        WHEREAS, Old Operator currently has the sole rights to act as operator of the Facility and wishes
to assign such rights to New Operator;

         WHEREAS, Old Operator currently owns certain tangible and intangible assets, including Supplies
(as defined below), furniture and equipment and other items of personal property, including as located on
or used in connection with, the operation of the Facility (as will not be transferred from Seller to Purchaser
pursuant to the APA, collectively, the “Personal Property”); and

         WHEREAS, in order to ensure an orderly transition of operations of the Facility, the Parties desire
to enter into this Agreement.

       NOW THEREFORE, in consideration of the mutual covenants and provisions contained herein,
and for other good and valuable consideration, the receipt and sufficiency of which are hereby
acknowledged, the Parties hereby covenant and agree as follows:

         Section 1.    Closing. The closing of the transactions contemplated by this Agreement (the
“Closing”) shall take place, subject to the satisfaction of the closing conditions set forth in Section 3 below,
simultaneously with the consummation of the transactions contemplated under the APA (such date on
which the Closing occurs, the “Closing Date”), and shall be effective as of 12:01 a.m. on the Closing Date.
New Operator shall use commercially reasonable efforts to obtain (i) approval from the Rhode Island
Department of Health (“RIDOH”) of a Change in Effective Control of the Facility to New Operator,
together with RIDOH’s written commitment to issue a license, effective as of the Closing Date, authorizing
New Operator to operate the Facility as a 120-bed skilled nursing facility under Rhode Island law
(collectively, the “CEC Approval”), and (ii) obtain all other licenses, certifications, permits, consents,
approvals and other assurances as are, under local custom and practice, customarily obtained by prudent
and reasonable operators of skilled nursing facilities similar to the Facility acting in good faith, before such
an operator takes possession of, and begins to operate, a facility similar to the Facility (collectively, with
the CEC Approval, the “Regulatory Approvals”).

         Section 2.     Due Diligence; Cooperation; Interim Operations of The Facility.

                 (a) Old Operator agrees to cooperate with New Operator, and New Operator agrees to
    cooperate with Old Operator to affect an orderly transfer of the operation of the Facility. From and
    after the Effective Date and through the Closing, Old Operator shall, during normal business hours and
    following advance notice from New Operator, provide New Operator and its members, employees,

    investors, lenders, agents and other representatives (collectively, “Representatives”) with access to the

    shall have the right to tour the Facility, to review the books and records related to the construction, use,
    occupancy, management, operation, maintenance, and financial condition thereof, interview residents
    of the Facility and employees of Old Operator and to observe the day-to-day operations and
    management of the Facility, provided that Old Operator, or Old Operator’s Representatives, shall have
    the right to accompany New Operator or any of New Operator’s Representatives during any such entry
    into the Facility.

                  (b) From the Effective Date until the earlier of the Closing Date and the date that this
    Agreement is terminated, Old Operator shall operate the Facility in the ordinary course of business,
    consistent with past practice and in accordance with all applicable laws, rules and regulations, use
    commercially reasonable and diligent efforts to preserve intact the business operations and relationships
    of the Facility with third parties and use best efforts to keep available the services of all of the Facility’s
    employees and consultants, including but not limited to administrators, directors of nursing and
    employees and consultants responsible for public relations and marketing. Without limiting the
    generality of the foregoing, until the earlier of (i) the Closing Date and (ii) the termination of this
    Agreement, Old Operator shall, unless consented to otherwise by New Operator in writing:

                          (i)     maintain, repair and replace where appropriate, consistent with past

         equipment, furniture and fixtures and leasehold improvements in substantially the same condition
         that exists on the date hereof, reasonable wear and tear excepted, and refrain from delaying such
         repair and/or replacement as a result of the pending transfer;

                          (ii)   maintain the Facility’s licensure status, Regulatory Approvals, and

         regulations;

                          (iii) exercise its commercially reasonable efforts to preserve Old Operator’s
         existing relationships with suppliers, distributors, customers and others having business relations
         with Old Operator;

                          (iv) maintain the Facility’s records in compliance with all applicable federal and
         state laws, ensuring that such records are consistently prepared, up-to-date, complete, accurate, and
         truthful;

         present residency occupancy levels of the Facility and the goodwill with all of the suppliers,
         residents and others having business relations with Old Operator or the Facility;

                          (vi) keep the Personal Property free and clear of all claims, liens, deeds of trust,
         mortgages, easements, restrictions, encumbrances, rights or interests of others, or security interests
         of any nature whatsoever (collectively, “Exceptions”) and promptly remove (or ensure removal of
         as of the Closing) any such Exceptions;

                          (vii) maintain all existing policies of insurance (or comparable policies) of or
         relating to the Facility or Personal Property in full force and effect;

         Section 4.     Conveyance; Tax Compliance Letter.

                (b) Without limitation to any other provision of this Agreement, the following items shall

         pertaining to or used in the operation of the Facility, stored in any form or medium, including any
         electronic data stored on computer disks or tapes, and originals of any of the foregoing that relate
         to the Facility;

         supplies, other supplies and food) located at and used in connection with the operation of the
         Facility (the “Supplies”), which Supplies shall be in a quantity consistent in all material respects
         with Old Operator’s historical practices in the ordinary course of business and in compliance with
         all applicable laws; and

                           (iii) Any and all other items of tangible and intangible personal property owned
         or leased (to the extent such lease is assumed by a New Operator hereunder) by Old Operator and

         furniture, fixtures and equipment, computer hardware, IT equipment, vehicles, and other items of

         Section 5.     Transfer of Patient Trust Funds.

                (a) On or prior to the Closing Date, Old Operator shall provide to New Operator a true,
    correct and complete accounting (properly reconciled) certified as being true, correct and complete by
    Old Operator of any patient trust funds and an inventory of all residents’ property held by Old Operator
    on the Closing Date for patients at the Facility (“Patient Trust Funds and Property”).

                 (b) Old Operator hereby agrees to transfer, or to cause to be transferred, to New Operator
    custody of the Patient Trust Funds and Property held by Old Operator on the Closing Date consistent
    with applicable law and regulations. New Operator hereby agrees that it will accept the Patient Trust
    Funds and Property in trust for the residents, in accordance with applicable statutory and regulatory
    requirements; provided, however, that such transfer shall not relieve Old Operator of its custodial and
    fiduciary responsibilities for such funds and property to the beneficiaries thereof for the period prior to
    the Closing Date.

                 (c) Old Operator hereby agrees to indemnify, defend and hold New Operator and its
    respective employees, Affiliates, managers, shareholders, officers, directors and agents, and each of
    their successors and permitted assigns (collectively, including New Operator, the “New Operator
    Parties”) harmless from all liabilities, claims, demands and causes of action of any nature whatsoever,
    including reasonable attorneys’ fees, in the event the amount of funds, if any, transferred to New
    Operator does not represent the full amount of the funds delivered to Old Operator as custodian or with
    respect to any Patient Trust Funds and Property delivered, or claimed to have been delivered, to Old
    Operator, but which were not delivered by Old Operator to New Operator, or for claims which arise

    from actions or omissions of Old Operator with respect to the Patient Trust Funds and Property prior

                 (d) New Operator will indemnify, defend and hold Old Operator and its employees,

    whatsoever, including reasonable attorneys’ fees, in the event a claim is made against Old Operator by

    Property actually received by New Operator.

         Section 6.     Provider Agreements; Cost Reports; Overpayments, Civil Monetary Penalties.

                 (a) At New Operator’s election, if New Operator is unable to obtain any of the Provider
    Agreements prior to the Closing Date, then, subject to applicable law, until such time that New Operator
    obtains such Provider Agreements, Old Operator shall authorize New Operator to use and bill under
    and obtain reimbursement under, such Provider Agreements of Old Operator, provided, however, that
    New Operator shall indemnify and hold Old Operator harmless on account of any liabilities caused by
    New Operator’s use of such Provider Agreements. Old Operator shall not cancel, or directly or
    indirectly cause the cancellation or limitation of, any of the Provider Agreements at any time from and
    after the Effective Date, including following the Closing Date, until the earlier of (i) the date on which
    New Operator has obtained replacement Provider Agreements in its own name or (ii) the date on which
    New Operator has consented in writing to such cancellation.

                 (b) Effective on the Closing Date, and subject at all times to applicable law, Old Operator
    sells, assigns and conveys to New Operator the Medicare and Medicaid provider number(s) in use at
    the Facility (the “Existing Provider Numbers”). Notwithstanding the foregoing, Old Operator retains
    any and all rights and liabilities relating to the Existing Provider Numbers relating to any and all periods
    preceding the Closing Date. Old Operator and New Operator shall execute any and all documents
    necessary for, and will otherwise cooperate in connection with, New Operator’s enrollment in the
    Medicare and Medicaid programs. During the pendency of New Operator’s CMS Form 855A, New
    Operator may bill Medicare and Medicaid under Old Operator’s name and the Existing Provider
    Numbers, until the intermediary changes the electronic funds transfer account or special payment
    address to the New Operator. Notwithstanding the foregoing, New Operator shall be responsible for
    all rights and liabilities relating to the Existing Provider Numbers relating to New Operator’s use
    thereof for any and all periods on or after the Closing Date. This Section 6(b) is intended to satisfy the
    requirements of Chapter Section 15.7.7.1.5 of the Medicare Program Integrity Manual.

                 (c) Old Operator shall prepare and file with the appropriate Medicare and Medicaid
    agencies its final cost reports with respect to its operation of the Facility as soon as reasonably
    practicable after the Closing Date, but in any event prior to the expiration of the period of time as may
    be required by law for the filing of each such final cost report under the applicable third-party payor
    program, it being specifically understood and agreed that the intent and purpose of this provision is to
    ensure that the reimbursement paid to New Operator for the period beginning on the Closing Date is
    not delayed, reduced or offset in any manner as a result of Old Operator’s failure to timely file such
    final cost reports.

                (d) Each Party agrees to notify the other within three (3) calendar days after receipt of
    any notice of any claim of recapture by RIDOH, Centers for Medicare and Medicaid Services (“CMS”),
    the Office of Inspector General for the United States Department of Health and Human Services
    (“OIG”) or any other governmental or quasi-governmental authority with respect to an alleged

4914-7428-3367, v. 18

===== PAGE 117 =====
    Medicare or Medicaid overpayment or any alleged underpayment of any tax or assessment for periods
    relating prior to the Closing Date (each, a “Recapture Claim”). To the extent ascertainable on or prior
    to the Closing Date, Old Operator shall pay or cause to be paid any Recapture Claim which relates to
    or arises from any period prior to the Closing Date; provided, however, that nothing herein shall be
    deemed to prevent or restrict Old Operator from contesting any such Recapture Claim, and, if, based
    on the advice of its attorneys, by paying such Recapture Claim, Old Operator shall have forfeited its
    right to such contest, Old Operator may delay paying such Recapture Claim, so long as Old Operator
    otherwise complies with the provisions of this Section 6, including paying any withheld funds as set
    forth in Section 6(e), in particular.

                (e) In the event that a Recapture Claim is made against the Facility for any period prior
    to the Closing Date, then Old Operator shall be entitled to contest such Recapture Claim; provided,
    however, that New Operator shall be allowed the opportunity to participate in all meetings, and be
    provided with copies of all audit adjustments and work papers. Old Operator and New Operator shall
    cooperate to resolve such audits to their mutual satisfaction. In the event that Old Operator fails to
    pursue any issue or issues raised in any such appeal, New Operator may, at its own expense, pursue an
    appeal of such issue or issues and Old Operator will cooperate fully with New Operator in such appeal,
    including by providing copies of any documentation required to substantiate costs reported on the cost
    reports. If Old Operator does not prevail with regard to such contest, Old Operator hereby agrees to
    save, indemnify, defend and hold the New Operator Parties harmless from and against any loss,
    damage, injury or expense incurred by any such party arising from or related to any such Recapture
    Claim. In connection with the foregoing indemnification obligation, in the event that CMS, RIDOH or
    any other governmental or quasi-governmental authority or agency or other third-party payor source
    withholds amounts from New Operator’s reimbursement checks or requires New Operator to pay any
    amounts as a result of such Recapture Claim, Old Operator shall pay such amounts to New Operator
    within five (5) days following New Operator’s demand therefor. Such payment shall be made
    regardless of whether Old Operator is then contesting such Recapture Claim.

                 (f) Old Operator shall be and remain obligated for and shall pay on or before the date due
    thereof all amounts of any license fees or taxes or other amounts payable to any other government
    authority with jurisdiction over the Facility accrued through the Closing Date, including any Medicaid
    provider taxes owed to RIDOH or state bed tax or assessment. Old Operator shall provide to New
    Operator, on or before the Closing Date, evidence reasonably satisfactory to New Operator of payment
    of all of such fees and taxes.

                  (g) In connection with violations or deficiencies cited by RIDOH in connection with any
    inspection or survey, whether conducted in the ordinary course or otherwise (collectively, a “Survey”),
    prior to, on, or after the Closing Date, Old Operator shall at its sole and exclusive cost and expense be
    liable and responsible for (i) the corrections detailed in the Statement of Deficiencies issued by RIDOH
    (the “Statement”), whether corrected prior to, on or after the Closing Date, (ii) all proposed or imposed
    remedies, including any CMP, and (iii) any proposed denial of payment by, or termination of
    certification to participate in, the Medicare or Medicaid programs set forth in the Statement or otherwise
    resulting from the Survey or the Statement (collectively, with Subsections (i) and (ii), the “Survey
    Results”), in each case, solely to the extent any such Survey Results are related to any conditions or
    incidents at the Facility prior to the Closing Date or from any actions or inactions of Old Operator.

                  (h) Old Operator shall deliver to New Operator copies of any Medicare and Medicaid
    cost reports for the Facility that have not been filed as of the Effective Date, for New Operator’s review,
    at least ten (10) days prior to filing of such reports, and provide New Operator with reasonable access
    to the underlying documentation for such reports.

    Operator shall not assume and shall not be liable, and nothing in this Agreement shall be construed as

    Personal Property (the “Retained Liabilities”), including, but not limited to the following: (i) liabilities
    or obligations of Old Operator to any of its creditors, shareholders, or owners; (ii) liabilities or
    obligations of Old Operator with respect to any acts, events or transactions occurring prior to, on or
    after the Closing Date; (iii) liabilities or obligations arising under any of the Rejected Contracts; (iv)
    liabilities or obligations arising under any contracts relating to any supplies or services delivered or
    provided to the Facility, and, in the case of the Assumed Contracts, delivered or provided to the Facility

    Facility prior to the Closing Date; (vi) liabilities or obligations of Old Operator for any federal, state,

    Operator, whether known or unknown by Old Operator or New Operator, (viii) liabilities or obligations
    relating to any employee benefit plans, pension plans, or other retirement arrangements, or relating to
    any collective bargaining agreement or union related matters; or (ix) liabilities or obligations related to
    or arising from Old Operator’s or the Facility’s receipt of Stimulus Funds. Unless specifically and

         Section 9.     Accounts Receivable; Accounts Payable.

                 (a) New Operator shall not assume any accounts receivable owed or due to Old Operator
    from or relating to the operation, ownership or management of the Facility prior to the Closing Date.
    Old Operator shall retain the right to collect all unpaid accounts receivable as of the close of business
    on the day prior to the Closing Date with respect to the Facility, but only to the extent that such accounts
    receivable relate to services rendered prior to the Closing Date. All collections shall be conducted in
    accordance with normal business practices and no patients or residents shall be unreasonably harassed
    in the collection of such amounts. Old Operator shall make commercially reasonable efforts to ensure
    that all Medicare and Medicaid billing will be submitted in a timely manner.

                 (b) If at any time after the Closing Date, New Operator shall receive any payment from
    any federal or state agency, which payment includes any reimbursement with respect to payments or
    underpayments made to Old Operator for services rendered prior to the Closing Date, then New
    Operator shall remit such payments to Old Operator. New Operator and Old Operator shall send copies
    of all Medicare and Medicaid remittance advices to the other Party for purposes of recording and
    pursuing accounts receivable for the period of twelve (12) months following the Closing Date and
    thereafter as reasonably requested by each Party. If at any time after the Closing Date, Old Operator
    shall receive any payment from any federal or state agency, which payment represents reimbursement
    with respect to payments or underpayments made to New Operator for services rendered on or after the
    Closing Date, then Old Operator shall remit such payments to New Operator. Any such remittances
    pursuant to this Section 9(b) shall occur within ten (10) days from the date the Party required to make
    such remittance receives payment thereof, and if not paid in full by such date, any amount outstanding
    shall bear interest at the Default Rate until paid in full.

                 (c) Payments received by New Operator or Old Operator from non-governmental
    payment sources shall be paid to the party designated in such payments entitled to the payments for the
    services provided thereunder within ten (10) days from the date the party required to make such
    remittance receives payment thereof, and if not paid in full by such date, any amount outstanding shall
    bear interest at the Default Rate until paid in full. Non-designated payments received by New Operator
    or Old Operator from non-governmental payments sources after the Closing Date shall (i) if received
    within forty five (45) days of the Closing Date, first be applied to any pre-Closing Date balances due

                                                      13


4914-7428-3367, v. 18

===== PAGE 120 =====
    to Old Operator for services provided prior the Closing Date, with the excess if any, applied to any
    post–Closing Date balances due for services rendered by New Operator after the Closing Date and (ii)
    if received at any time after forty five (45) days following the Closing Date, first be applied to any post-
    Closing Date monthly balances due to New Operator for services provided after the Closing Date, with
    the excess if any, applied to any pre–Closing Date monthly balances due for services rendered by Old
    Operator prior to the Closing Date. Notwithstanding the foregoing, New Operator hereby
    acknowledges and agrees that such pre-Closing Date monthly balances are the property of Old Operator
    and Old Operator reserves the right to continue to directly pursue the collection of such pre-Closing
    Date monthly balances.

                 (d) To the extent a Party receives any payments for accounts receivable of another Party,
    the Parties acknowledge that the Party receiving the payment belonging to another Party shall hold the
    payment in trust, that no Party shall have any right to offset with respect to such accounts receivable,
    and that the Party erroneously receiving the payment shall have no right, title or interest whatsoever in
    the payment and shall remit the same to the appropriate other Party within ten (10) days of receipt
    thereof, and if not paid in full by such date, any amount outstanding shall bear interest at the Default
    Rate until paid in full.

                (e) Nothing herein shall be deemed to limit in any way any Party’s rights and remedies
    to recover accounts receivable due and owing to it under the terms of this Agreement.

                 (f) All accounts payable for services provided or goods furnished for or at the Facility
    prior to the Closing Date, notwithstanding whether such accounts payable were incurred in the name
    of Old Operator, shall remain the sole responsibility and obligation of Old Operator. All accounts
    payable for services provided or goods furnished for or at the Facility on or after the Closing Date,
    notwithstanding whether such accounts payable were incurred in the name of New Operator (except
    with respect to Rejected Contracts), shall be the sole responsibility and obligation of New Operator.
    To the extent accounts payable have accrued for a period that includes time both before and after the
    Closing Date, the Parties shall equitably apportion the responsibility for payment of such accounts
    payable. The Parties hereby agree to cooperate with each other and to notify the merchants, suppliers
    or other third parties with respect to which of Old Operator or New Operator bears responsibility for
    accounts payable of the Facility based on the foregoing clauses of this Section 9(f).

         Section 10.    Employees.

                 (a) Old Operator shall terminate the employment of all employees providing services at
    the Facility, a listing of which is attached hereto as Schedule 10(a) (such Schedule to include the
    position and current base salary of each such employee) (each, a “Current Employee” and collectively,
    the “Current Employees”), effective as of the Closing Date. Old Operator shall be responsible for any
    “Continuation Coverage” (as that term is defined by COBRA Section 4980B of the tax code and Section
    601, et seq. of ERISA) for any employee of Old Operator terminated at any time prior to or on the
    Closing Date who does not become a Retained Employee (as defined below). New Operator shall not
    be bound by or assume any employment contracts to which Old Operator may be a party.

                  (b) New Operator shall determine, in its sole discretion, which of the Current Employees
    shall be offered employment with New Operator, pursuant to employment terms acceptable to New
    Operator (each, a “Retained Employee” and collectively, the “Retained Employees”). Notwithstanding
    the foregoing, New Operator shall rehire or offer to rehire not less than the number of the Current
    Employees necessary to avoid WARN Act notice or similar notice requirements or other related
    liabilities contained in any applicable law. Notwithstanding the foregoing, nothing in this Section 10(b)
    shall create any right in favor of any person not a party hereto, including the Current Employees, or

===== PAGE 121 =====
    constitute an employment agreement or condition of employment for any employee of Old Operator or
    any Affiliate of Old Operator who is a Current Employee or Retained Employee.

                 (c) On the date of the Closing, Old Operator shall provide, or shall cause Seller to provide,
    New Operator and Purchaser with a credit to the Purchase Price (as defined in the APA) under the APA
    in an amount equal to sixty percent (60%) of the accrued, vested and unvested, but unpaid bonuses and
    vacation, sick, bereavement, holiday pay and severance obligations, plus all applicable employer
    withholding charges and payroll costs (including, without limitation, FICA, FUTA, SUTA, worker’s
    compensation and union dues, if any) and costs associated with processing payroll, as well as any
    insurance premium obligations of Old Operator, vested or unvested, with respect to the Retained
    Employees that have accrued prior to the date of the Closing (“Old Operator’s Vacation and Holiday
    Pay Expenses”). New Operator agrees to carry over all of such Old Operator’s Vacation and Holiday
    Pay Expenses for the benefit of, and as applicable to, each Retained Employee, provided, however, that
    New Operator shall not be liable for, and Old Operator shall indemnify and hold the New Operator
    Parties harmless, on account of any and all other liabilities and obligations with regard to any of the
    Current Employees and with regard to the Retained Employees, any and all other liabilities and
    obligations that shall have accrued prior to the Closing Date. A schedule of Old Operator’s Vacation
    and Holiday Pay Expenses is attached hereto as Schedule 10(c). If either party discovers after the date
    of the Closing that the amount credited is more or less than the amounts required to be credited under
    this Section 10(c) then, (i) in the event of a deficit, Old Operator shall pay to New Operator within five
    (5) days after New Operator provides notice thereof, by an amount equal to such deficiencies, and (ii)
    in the event of an overage, New Operator shall pay to Old Operator within five (5) days after discovery
    by an amount equal to such deficiencies.

                 (d) New Operator and Old Operator acknowledge and agree that the employees at the
    Facility provide valuable services that are crucial for the success of the Facility, and New Operator’s
    decision to serve as licensed operator of the Facility is based upon the skills and qualifications of such
    employees. As such, in the event that during the period beginning on the Effective Date and ending
    upon the date that is eighteen (18) months following the Closing Date any Current Employee is solicited
    for employment, or in the event that during the period beginning on the Effective Date and ending upon
    the date that is eight (8) months following the Closing Date any Current Employee that is hired by any
    Affiliate of Old Operator (any of the foregoing, an “Old Operator Party”), then Old Operator shall pay
    to New Operator an amount equal to the greater of (a) $60,000.00, or (b) an amount equal to such
    employee’s annual salary as liquidated damages, for each such Current Employee. The Parties
    acknowledge and agree that actual damages with respect to the foregoing would be difficult to ascertain
    and that the amount set forth in the immediately preceding sentence is a fair and reasonable
    approximation of such actual damages and shall be the sole remedy of New Operator. Old Operator
    further acknowledges that the scope and duration of the provisions of this Section 10(d) are reasonable.
    The Parties acknowledge and agree that advertisements available to the general public, such as through
    website job postings and newspaper, Internet and trade journals, or non-targeted social media posts
    shall not constitute solicitation for purposes of this Section 10(d).

                 (e) No later than the thirtieth (30th) day following the Closing Date, Old Operator shall
    fulfill any and all of its vesting obligations under pension or 401K plans, with respect to the Retained
    Employees, and shall provide proof to New Operator of the same.

                (f) At or prior to the Closing, Old Operator shall pay any amounts due or to become due
    to any Current Employees pursuant to any change-in-control, retention, severance or similar agreements
    or plans.

         Section 12.    Access to Records.

                  (a) Without limiting the provisions of Section 20, on the Closing Date, Old Operator shall
    deliver to New Operator all (i) records of the Facility (including with respect to residents and
    employees) other than medical records, (ii) resident medical records for then-current residents of the
    Facility as well as those medical records for all residents of the Facility at any time between the date
    that is sixty (60) days prior to the Closing Date and the Closing Date and (iii) MDS submissions made
    by the facility for the period between the date that is eighteen (18) months prior to the Closing Date
    and the Closing Date; provided, however, that nothing herein shall be construed as precluding Old
    Operator from removing from the Facility on the Closing Date its corporate financial records which
    relate to its operations at the Facility or to its overall corporate operations; provided, further, that
    following the Closing, Old Operator shall give New Operator access to any information and the right
    of inspection (including the right to extract or make copies) in any such removed records as is necessary
    for the efficient and lawful operation of the Facility by New Operator or is otherwise required by law
    to be maintained at the Facility.

                  (b) Subsequent to the Closing Date, New Operator shall allow Old Operator and its agents
    and representatives to have reasonable access to (upon reasonable prior notice and during normal
    business hours), and to make copies of, the books and records and supporting material of the Facility
    relating to the period prior to and including the Closing Date, at its own expense, to the extent
    reasonably necessary to enable Old Operator to investigate and defend malpractice, employee or other
    claims, to file or defend cost reports and tax returns or any other government agency actions or notices.

                  (c) Old Operator shall, if allowed by applicable law and subject to the terms of such
    applicable law, be entitled to remove and/or copy any records delivered to New Operator, for purposes
    of litigation involving a resident or employee to whom such record relates, as certified to New Operator
    in writing prior to removal by an officer of or counsel for Old Operator in connection with such
    threatened or actual litigation. Any record so removed shall promptly be returned to New Operator (or,
    at New Operator’s election, destroyed) following its use.

                 (d) New Operator agrees to maintain such books, records and other material comprising
    records of the Facility’s operations prior to the Closing Date that have been received by New Operator
    from Old Operator or otherwise, including patient records and records of patient funds, to the extent
    required by law.

         Section 15.    Taxes.

                 (a) Old Operator shall discharge any provider or other tax charged by RIDOH or other
    government agency relating to any period prior to the Closing Date. Old Operator shall file all returns,
    reports and filings of any kind or nature, required to be filed by Old Operator on a timely basis and will

    to the Personal Property or the Facility, in the ordinary course of business. Old Operator shall pay
    before the same shall become due all taxes, duties and other governmental charges that accrue prior to
    the Closing Date, which, if not paid, would create or may hereafter create a lien on any of the assets of
    New Operator or for which New Operator could become liable as a successor operator of the Facility.
    Old Operator shall also file any required bulk transfer filings necessary to establish that New Operator
    shall not succeed to any state tax liabilities.

                 (b) New Operator shall notify Old Operator of any pending tax appeal or assessment or
    claim in any audit, litigation or other proceeding, in each case relating to any taxes related to the Facility
    or any Personal Property for any taxable period (a “Straddle Period”) that includes but does not end on
    the Closing Date (a “Tax Contest”). New Operator shall have the right to control any such Tax
    Contests, including the settlement or other disposition thereof, provided, that (i) Old Operator shall
    have the right to participate, at its own expense, in any such Tax Contests that may give rise to an
    indemnification claim pursuant to this Agreement and (ii) New Operator shall consider in good faith
    any reasonable comments of Old Operator with respect to the conduct of such Tax Contests. Without
    limiting the foregoing, New Operator shall have the sole right to initiate (where applicable) and control
    each Tax Contest.

         Section 16.    Indemnification.

                  (a) New Operator shall indemnify, save, protect, defend and hold harmless Old Operator
    and its respective employees, Affiliates, managers, members, partners, officers, directors and agents,
    from and against all claims, liabilities, losses, damages, demands and causes of action of any nature
    whatsoever (including demands and causes of action relating to injury or death to persons or loss of or
    damage to property), and all costs and expenses (including penalties and reasonable attorneys’ and
    other professional fees and disbursements incurred in the investigation or defense of any such claims,
    or in asserting, pursuing or enforcing any such claims), whether or not resulting from third-party claims
    (collectively, “Losses”) arising from, out of, or relating to (i) operation of the Facility by New Operator
    on or after the Closing Date, (ii) New Operator’s use and occupancy of the Real Property and use of
    the Personal Property, and the condition thereof on or after the Closing Date, and (iii) any inaccuracy
    or breach of any representation, warranty, covenant, agreement or obligation of New Operator
    contained in this Agreement or in any of the Other Documents.

                 (b) Subject to the terms in Section 12 of the APA, Old Operator shall indemnify, save,
    protect, defend and hold harmless each of the New Operator Parties from and against all Losses arising
    from, out of, or relating to (i) the ownership or operation of the Facility by Old Operator prior to the
    Closing Date, (ii) Old Operator’s use and occupancy of the Real Property and use of the Personal
    Property, and the condition thereof prior to the Closing Date, (iii) any Recapture Claim, (iv) any
    inaccuracy or breach of any representation, warranty, covenant, agreement or obligation of Old
    Operator contained in this Agreement or in any of the Other Documents, and (v) the Retained
    Liabilities.


                                                       17


4914-7428-3367, v. 18

===== PAGE 124 =====
                 (c) In the event that any liability, claim or demand for Losses which are subject to
    indemnification by or under any provision of this Agreement (“Indemnitee’s Claim”) is made against
    or received by any indemnified party (hereinafter “Indemnitee”) hereunder, such Indemnitee shall
    notify the indemnifying party (hereinafter “Indemnitor”) in writing within thirty (30) calendar days of
    Indemnitee’s receipt of written notice thereof; provided, however, that Indemnitee’s failure to timely
    notify Indemnitor of Indemnitee’s receipt of an Indemnitee’s Claim shall not impair, void, vitiate or
    invalidate Indemnitee’s right to receive, and Indemnitor’s obligation to provide, indemnification
    hereunder unless such failure directly and materially prejudices Indemnitor’s right or ability to defend
    the Indemnitee’s Claim. Upon receipt of notice of any Indemnitee’s Claim(s), Indemnitor shall, subject
    to Indemnitees other rights as set forth herein, diligently and vigorously defend, compromise or settle
    said Indemnitee’s Claim at Indemnitor’s sole and exclusive cost and expense and shall promptly
    provide Indemnitee evidence thereof promptly upon the final, unappealable resolution of such
    Indemnitee’s Claim. Indemnitor shall keep Indemnitee informed with respect to all aspects of the
    resolution of an Indemnitee’s Claim. Without limiting the foregoing, upon request by Indemnitee,
    Indemnitor shall within two (2) calendar days (A) provide Indemnitee a true, correct, accurate and
    complete written status report regarding the then current status of any Indemnitee’s Claim and (B) file
    for, and vigorously pursue, the removal as a defendant of any Indemnitee who is improperly named in
    any action underlying an Indemnitee’s Claim. Indemnitor shall not, without the prior written consent
    of Indemnitee, which consent shall not be unreasonably withheld, settle, compromise or offer to settle
    or compromise any Indemnitee’s Claim on a basis that would result in (i) the imposition of a consent
    order, injunction or decree that would restrict the future activity or conduct of Indemnitee or any
    Affiliate thereof, or (ii) any monetary liability on the part of Indemnitee or any Affiliate thereof that
    will not be paid or reimbursed by the Indemnitor. Prior to an Indemnification Default (as defined
    herein), Indemnitee may not settle or compromise an Indemnitee’s Claim without Indemnitor’s prior
    written consent. In the event that Indemnitor fails or refuses to indemnify, save, defend, protect or hold
    Indemnitee harmless from and against an Indemnitee’s Claim or to diligently pursue the same to its
    conclusion, or in the event that Indemnitor fails to timely report to Indemnitee the status of its efforts
    to reach a final resolution of an Indemnitee’s Claim, on seven (7) calendar days prior written notice to
    Indemnitor during which time Indemnitor may cure any alleged default hereunder, the foregoing shall
    immediately, automatically and without further notice be deemed an event of default hereunder (an
    “Indemnification Default”) and thereafter Indemnitee may, but shall not be obligated to, immediately
    and without notice to Indemnitor, except such notice as may be required by law, intervene in and
    defend, settle or compromise such Indemnitee’s Claim at Indemnitor’s sole and exclusive cost and
    expense, including but not limited to attorneys’ fees, and, thereafter, within seven (7) calendar days of
    written demand thereof, Indemnitor shall promptly reimburse Indemnitee all amounts (including
    attorneys’ fees) incurred by Indemnitee with respect to the resolution of such Indemnitee’s Claims.

                 (d) If and when an Indemnitee desires to assert an Indemnitee’s Claim in accordance with
    Section 16(a) or Section 16(b), as applicable, other than with respect to a third-party claim in
    accordance with Section 16(c), Indemnitee shall notify Indemnitor of such Indemnitee’s Claim and the
    Parties shall attempt in good faith to agree on resolution of the disputed amount. Any amount mutually
    agreed upon or awarded to the Indemnitee under a final judgment shall be paid by Indemnitor within
    five (5) business days following such agreement or the entering of such judgment, as applicable.

                (e) Other than where a different survival period is expressly set forth, all indemnification
    obligations of Old Operator and New Operator under this Agreement shall survive the Closing Date
    and shall continue in effect for a period of eighteen months (18) months after the Closing Date;
    provided, however, that if there is an Indemnitee’s Claim made prior to any applicable survival period,
    such indemnification obligation shall continue to survive until the final, non-appealable resolution of
    such Indemnitee’s Claim.

        Section 19. Non-Solicitation. No Old Operator Party shall solicit any residents of the Facility for
a period of two (2) years following the Closing. Old Operator acknowledges that if there is a violation of
any provision of this Section 19, then Old Operator shall pay to New Operator an amount equal to
$50,000.00 as liquidated damages, for each such resident. The Parties acknowledge and agree that actual
damages with respect to the foregoing would be difficult to ascertain and that $50,000.00 is a fair and
reasonable approximation of such actual damages and shall be the sole remedy of New Operator. Old
Operator further acknowledges that the scope and duration of the provisions of this Section 19 are
reasonable.

         Section 20.    Information Systems.

                 (a) Without limitation of Section 12, Old Operator shall use reasonable efforts to permit
    transfer of current data in fully operational form for use in New Operator’s computer applications. Old
    Operator further agrees that in order to assist New Operator in ensuring the continued operation of the
    Facility after the Closing Date in compliance with applicable law and in a manner which does not
    jeopardize the health and welfare of the residents of the Facility, Old Operator shall, for a period of no
    longer than ninety (90) days after the Closing Date, provide New Operator access to Old Operator’s
    electronic medical records system to enable New Operator, at its expense, to print the medical treatment
    records and physician orders for each resident as of the Closing Date that was a resident as of the period
    between and including the Closing Date and the date that is eighteen (18) months prior to the Closing
    Date, and cooperate with New Operator regarding the delivery of all such records to New Operator, in
    electronic form (including the provision to New Operator of the last eighteen (18) months of MDS
    (Minimum Data Set) history in the format submitted to CMS by Old Operator), in order to enable New
    Operator to obtain the necessary copies of such medical records and physician orders.

                 (b) Prior to the Closing, Old Operator shall provide New Operator and its representatives
    with access to the Facility so that New Operator can install lines necessary for computer hardware,
    together with servers, computer hardware and software. In addition, Old Operator shall cooperate with
    New Operator and provide New Operator with such assistance as New Operator may reasonably request
    in order to provide for an orderly, efficient and safe transition of the operations from Old Operator to
    New Operator and the continued operation of the Facility after the Closing Date in compliance with
    applicable law and in a manner which does not jeopardize the health and welfare of the residents of the
    Facility. During such time as Old Operator provides New Operator with access to the Facility under
    this Section 20(b), New Operator shall not connect with or hook into Old Operator’s computer lines or
    computers. Such access shall be prescheduled with Old Operator and shall not interrupt normal
    business operations. New Operator shall indemnify Old Operator for any damage resulting from such
    access. If, for any reason, this Agreement terminates prior to the Closing Date, Old Operator shall
    remove any lines installed, at New Operator’s cost and expense, and New Operator shall remit payment
    to Old Operator within five (5) days of receiving an invoice for such costs and expenses of removal.
